\displaystyle x\in B_{3r/4}

and
\displaystyle y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r}
implies
\displaystyle 1\leq\frac{|y-x_{0}|}{|x-y|}\leq 4
and then recalling
\displaystyle\psi
is supported in
\displaystyle B_{3r/4}
we get using Young’s inequality

{}\begin{array}[]{l}\quad\fint_{B_{r}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{q-1}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dy\right)|u(x)-(u)_{B_{r}}|\psi^{p}dx\\
\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\fint_{B_{r}}|u(x)-(u)_{B_{r}}|dx\\
\leq Cr^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\times r^{-s}\left(\fint_{B_{r}}|u(x)-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{q}dx\right)^{1/q}\\
\leq Cr^{-sq}\fint_{B_{r}}|u-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{q}dx+C\left(r^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}},\end{array}

(4.9)

for some
\displaystyle C\equiv C(n,p,q,s)
.

Estimate of
\displaystyle V
:

As
\displaystyle f\in L^{n}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
and
\displaystyle 0\leq\psi\leq 1
, so

{}\begin{array}[]{rcl}\fint_{B_{r}}|f||u-(u)_{B_{r}}|\psi^{p}dx&\leq&C\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}\left(\fint_{B_{r}}|\psi^{p}(u-(u)_{B_{r}})|^{p^{*}}dx\right)^{1/p^{*}}\\
&\leq&C\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}\left(\fint_{B_{r}}|\nabla(\psi^{p}(u-(u)_{B_{r}}))|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}\\
&\leq&C\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}\left[\left(\fint_{B_{r}}(p\psi^{p-1}|\nabla\psi||u-(u)_{B_{r}}|)^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}+\left(\fint_{B_{r}}\psi^{p^{2}}|\nabla u|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}\right]\\
&\leq&C\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}r^{-1}\left(\fint_{B_{r}}|u-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}+C\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}\left(\fint_{B_{r}}\psi^{p}|\nabla u|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}\\
&\leq&C\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+Cr^{-p}\fint_{B_{r}}|u-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{p}dx+C\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+\frac{1}{2}\fint_{B_{r}}\psi^{p}|\nabla u|^{p}dx,\end{array}

(4.10)

for some
\displaystyle C\equiv C(n,p)
. Finally for
\displaystyle r<1
, it holds

{}r^{-sq}[\operatorname{av}_{q}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{q}\leq cr^{(1-s)q}(r^{-p}[\operatorname{av}_{p}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{p})^{q/p}\leq c(r^{-p}[\operatorname{av}_{p}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{p}+1).

(4.11)

In view of
Eq.

4.11
, merging
Eqs.

4.8
,
4.9
and
4.10
and using in
Eq.

4.7
, we get

{}\begin{array}[]{l}\fint_{B_{r/2}(x_{0})}|\nabla u|^{p}dx+\fint_{B_{r/2}(x_{0})}\int_{B_{r/2}(x_{0})}\frac{|u(x)-u(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+qs}}dxdy\\
\leq C\left(r^{-p}[\operatorname{av}_{p}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{p}+r^{-\delta}[\operatorname{snail}_{\delta}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{q}+\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+1\right).\end{array}

(4.12)

We now define
\displaystyle h\in u+W_{0}^{1,p}(B_{r/4}(x_{0}))
as the (unique) solution to

{}-\operatorname{div}A(x_{0},\nabla h(x))=0\text{ in }B_{r/4}(x_{0}),\quad h=u\text{ on }\partial B_{r/4}(x_{0}).

(4.13)

The function
\displaystyle h
solves the Euler-Lagrange equation

{}\int_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}A(x_{0},\nabla h)\cdot\nabla\varphi\,dx=0\quad\text{ for every }\varphi\in W_{0}^{1,p}(B_{r/4}).

(4.14)

The following lemma includes some crucial properties satisfied by
\displaystyle h
that we will eventually pass to
\displaystyle u
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
\displaystyle h
be a solution to
Eq.

4.13
. Then there exists constants
\displaystyle\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
, and
\displaystyle C,c>0
such that

{}\fint_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx\leq C\fint_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}(|\nabla u|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx,

(4.15)

{}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r/4}(x_{0}))}\leq\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r/4}(x_{0}))},\quad\underset{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}{\operatorname{osc}}h\leq\underset{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}{\operatorname{osc}}u,

(4.16)

and

{}\|\nabla h\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r/8}(x_{0}))}^{p}\leq c\fint_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx,\quad\underset{B_{t}(x_{0})}{\operatorname{osc}}\nabla h\leq c\left(\frac{t}{r}\right)^{\alpha_{0}}\left(\fint_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx\right)^{1/p}

(4.17)

holds for all
\displaystyle t\in(0,r/8]
.

Proof.

We only prove
Eq.

4.15
, as
Eq.

4.16
follows from the standard maximum principle of the elliptic operator
\displaystyle h\mapsto A(x_{0},\nabla h)
and
Eq.

4.17
follows from [
44
, Theorem 1, Theorem 2]. To prove
Eq.

4.15
, we test
Eq.

4.14
with
\displaystyle u-h\in W_{0}^{1,p}(B_{r/4}({x}_{0}))
and use
Eq.

2.6
to get

\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}A(x_{0},\nabla h)\cdot\nabla h\,dx=\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}A(x_{0},\nabla h)\cdot\nabla u\,dx\leq\Lambda\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|\nabla h||\nabla u|dx.

Using again hypothesis
Eq.

2.6
, we see that
\displaystyle A({x}_{0},z)\cdot z\geq\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{p-1}\right\}\Lambda^{-1}(|z|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|z|^{2}
for every
\displaystyle z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, so that

\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}A(x_{0},\nabla h)\cdot\nabla h\,dx\geq\frac{{\Lambda}^{-1}}{p}\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|\nabla h|^{2}dx.

Thus,

{}\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|\nabla h|^{2}dx\leq p\Lambda^{2}\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|\nabla h||\nabla u|dx.

(4.18)

Now, if
\displaystyle p\geq 2
this yields

\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}|\nabla h|^{p}dx\leq p{\Lambda}^{2}\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}dx.

which immediately gives
Eq.

4.15
after an application of Holder’s inequality. If
\displaystyle p\in(1,2)
, we also exploit Hölder’s inequality along with the fact that

\frac{t^{\frac{p}{p-1}}}{(t^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{(2-p)p}{2(p-1)}}}\leq(t^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}t^{2},\quad\text{ for all }t\geq 0

to deduce from
Eq.

4.18
that

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|\nabla h|^{2}dx&\leq&p{\Lambda}^{2}\left(\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}\frac{|\nabla h|^{\frac{p}{p-1}}}{(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{(2-p)p}{2(p-1)}}}dx\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}|\nabla u|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\\
&\leq&p{\Lambda}^{2}\left(\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|\nabla h|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}|\nabla u|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.\end{array}

This gives

\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|\nabla h|^{2}dx\leq 2^{p}\Lambda^{2p}\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}|\nabla u|^{p}dx\leq 2^{p}\Lambda^{2p}\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}(|\nabla u|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx

which, together with the trivial estimate

\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\mu^{2}dx\leq\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}\mu^{p}dx\leq\int_{B_{r/4}({x}_{0})}(|\nabla u|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx

readily yields
Eq.

4.15
.
∎

Next, we give the difference estimate to pass the regularity of
\displaystyle h
to
\displaystyle u
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\displaystyle h\in u+W_{0}^{1,p}(B_{r/4}(x_{0})),0<r<1
be the solution of
Eq.

4.13
. There exists
\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\equiv\sigma_{0}(p,s,q,\bar{\alpha})\in

\displaystyle(0,1)
such that

{}\fint_{B_{r/4}}|u-h|^{p}dx\leq cr^{\theta\sigma_{0}}\left[\fint_{B_{r}}|u-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{p}dx+r^{\delta}\left(r^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}+r^{p-\theta}(\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+1)\right],

(4.19)

holds for every
\displaystyle\theta\in(0,1)
where
\displaystyle\delta\in(sq,p)
,
\displaystyle c\equiv c(n,p,q,s,\Lambda)
.

Proof.

Consider the vector field
\displaystyle V_{\mu}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
, defined by

\displaystyle V_{\mu}(z):=(|z|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{(p-2)/4}z,

whenever
\displaystyle z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where
\displaystyle p\in(1,\infty)
and
\displaystyle\mu\in[0,1]
. It follows that

{}|V_{\mu}(z_{1})-V_{\mu}(z_{2})|\approx(|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{(p-2)/4}|z_{1}-z_{2}|,

(4.20)

where the equivalence holds up to constants depending only on
\displaystyle n,p
. A standard consequence of
Eq.

2.6
3
is the following strict monotonicity inequality:

{}|V_{\mu}(z_{1})-V_{\mu}(z_{2})|^{2}\leq c(A(x,z_{2})-A(x,z_{1}))\cdot(z_{2}-z_{1})

(4.21)

holds for all
\displaystyle x\in\Omega
whenever
\displaystyle z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where
\displaystyle c\equiv c(n,p,\Lambda)
. The last two inequalities are, however, based on the following one

\int_{0}^{1}(|z_{1}+\lambda(z_{2}-z_{1})|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{t/2}d\lambda\approx_{n,t}(|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{t/2}

that holds whenever
\displaystyle t>-1
and
\displaystyle z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
are such that
\displaystyle|z_{1}|+|z_{2}|+\mu>0
. Noting [
24
, Section 2.5], we now choose
\displaystyle u-h\in W_{0}^{1,p}(B_{r/4}({x}_{0}))
as a test function in the weak formulation of both
\displaystyle u
and
\displaystyle v
and use
Eq.

4.21
to get

{}\begin{array}[]{rcl}\fint_{B_{r/4}}|V_{\mu}(\nabla u)-V_{\mu}(\nabla h)|^{2}dx&\leq&c\fint_{B_{r/4}}(A(x_{0},\nabla u)-A(x_{0},\nabla h))\cdot(\nabla u-\nabla h)dx\\
&=&c\fint_{B_{r/4}}A(x_{0},\nabla u)\cdot(\nabla u-\nabla h)dx\\
&=&c\fint_{B_{r/4}}(A(x_{0},\nabla u)-A(x,\nabla u))\cdot(\nabla u-\nabla h)dx+c\fint_{B_{r/4}}f(u-h)dx\\
&&-c\fint_{B_{r/2}}\int_{B_{r/2}}\frac{|u(x)-u(y)|^{q-2}(u(x)-u(y))((u-h)(x)-(u-h)(y))}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\\
&&-2c\fint_{B_{r/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r/2}}\frac{|u(x)-u(y)|^{q-2}(u(x)-u(y))(u-h)(x)}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dydx\\
&=&(\mathrm{I})+(\mathrm{II})+(\mathrm{III})+(\mathrm{IV}),\end{array}

(4.22)

where
\displaystyle c\equiv c(n,p,\Lambda)
. In view of [
24
, Lemma 4.2], it is enough to estimate
\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})
and
\displaystyle(\mathrm{IV})
. Using
Eq.

2.6
2
, we get

{}\begin{array}[]{rcl}(\mathrm{I})&=&\fint_{B_{r/4}}(A(x_{0},\nabla u)-A(x,\nabla u))\cdot(\nabla u-\nabla h)dx\leq Cr^{\bar{\alpha}}\fint_{B_{r/4}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-1}{2}}|\nabla u-\nabla h|dx\\
&\leq&Cr^{\bar{\alpha}}\left(\fint_{B_{r/4}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}dx\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\fint_{B_{r/4}}|\nabla u-\nabla h|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{caccimprovedfinal},\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{h1}}}{{\leq}}&Cr^{\bar{\alpha}}\left(r^{-p}[\operatorname{av}_{p}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{p}+r^{-\delta}[\operatorname{snail}_{\delta}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{q}+\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+1\right),\end{array}

(4.23)

for some
\displaystyle C\equiv C(n,p,q,s,\Lambda)
. For
\displaystyle(\mathrm{IV})
we note that we can replace
\displaystyle u
by
\displaystyle u-(u)_{B_{r/2}}
and use that
\displaystyle x\in B_{r/4},y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r/2}
imply
\displaystyle|y-x_{0}|/|x-y|\leq 2
. Recalling that
\displaystyle u-h
is supported in
\displaystyle B_{r/4}
, we then have using
Eqs.

4.2
,
4.3
and
4.1

{}\begin{array}[]{l}(\mathrm{IV})\leq C\fint_{B_{r/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r/2}}\frac{\operatorname{max}\{|u(x)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q-1},|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q-1}\}|u(x)-h(x)|}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dydx\\
\leq C\fint_{B_{r/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r/2}}\frac{\operatorname{max}\{|u(x)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q-1},|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q-1}\}|u(x)-h(x)|}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dydx,\\
\leq Cr^{-sq}\fint_{B_{r/2}}|u(x)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q-1}|u(x)-h(x)|dx\\
+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r/2}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\times\fint_{B_{r/2}}|u(x)-h(x)|dx\\
\leq C\left[r^{-sq}\left(\fint_{B_{r/2}}|u(x)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q}\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r/2}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right]\\
\times\left(\fint_{B_{r/2}}|u(x)-h(x)|^{q}dx\right)^{1/q}\\
\leq C\left[\left(r^{-sq}\fint_{B_{r/2}}|u(x)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q}\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}+r^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r/2}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right]\\
\times\left(r^{-sq}\fint_{B_{r/2}}|u(x)-h(x)|^{q}dx\right)^{1/q}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{ccp}}}{{\leq}}C\left([r^{-p}\operatorname{av}_{p}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{p}+r^{-\delta}[\operatorname{snail}_{\delta}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{q}+1\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\left(r^{-sq}\fint_{B_{r/2}}|u(x)-h(x)|^{q}dx\right)^{1/q},\end{array}

(4.24)

for some
\displaystyle C\equiv C(n,p,q,s,\Lambda)
. Now by Sobolev embedding
Theorem

2.11
, one has

\begin{array}[]{c}\left(r^{-sq}\fint_{B_{r/2}}|u-h|^{q}dx\right)^{1/q}\leq Cr^{(1-s)}\left(r^{-p}\fint_{B_{r/2}}|u-h|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}\leq Cr^{(1-s)}\left(\fint_{B_{r/2}}|\nabla u-\nabla h|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{caccimprovedfinal},\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{h1}}}{{\leq}}Cr^{(1-s)}\left(r^{-p}[\operatorname{av}_{p}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{p}+r^{-\delta}[\operatorname{snail}_{\delta}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{q}+\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+1\right)^{1/p},\end{array}

for some
\displaystyle C\equiv C(n,p,q,s,\Lambda)
. This along with
Eq.

4.24
gives

{}(\mathrm{IV})\leq Cr^{1-s}\left(r^{-p}[\operatorname{av}_{p}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{p}+r^{-\delta}[\operatorname{snail}_{\delta}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{q}+\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+1\right)^{1-1/q+1/p},

(4.25)

for some
\displaystyle C\equiv C(n,p,q,s,\Lambda)
. Denoting
\displaystyle\operatorname{ccp}(r)=r^{-p}[\operatorname{av}_{p}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{p}+r^{-\delta}[\operatorname{snail}_{\delta}(u,B_{r}(x_{0}))]^{q}+\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+1
and then using
Eq.

4.23
,
Eq.

4.25
along with the estimates of other terms from [
24
, Lemma 4.2], we get from
Eq.

4.22

{}\begin{array}[]{l}\fint_{B_{r/4}}|V_{\mu}(\nabla u)-V_{\mu}(\nabla h)|^{2}dx\leq Cr^{\bar{\alpha}\operatorname{min}\{1,p/2\}}\operatorname{ccp}(r)+Cr^{1-s}\operatorname{ccp}(r)^{1-1/q+1/p}+C\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}\operatorname{ccp}(r)^{1/p},\end{array}

(4.26)

for some
\displaystyle C\equiv C(n,p,q,s,\Lambda)
. As
\displaystyle r<1
and
\displaystyle p>\delta
, so for each
\displaystyle\theta\in(0,1)
,

r^{p}\operatorname{ccp}(r)\leq\left[\fint_{B_{r}}|u-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{p}dx+r^{\delta}\left(r^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}+r^{p-\theta}(\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+1)\right].

Using this, one can now estimate from
Eq.

4.26
like [
24
, Lemma 4.2, Pages 29-30], to get

\begin{array}[]{l}\fint_{B_{r/4}}|u-h|^{p}dx\leq Cr^{\bar{\alpha}\operatorname{min}\{1,p/2\}}r^{p}\operatorname{ccp}(r)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad+Cr^{\theta\sigma}\left[\fint_{B_{r}}|u-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{p}dx+r^{\delta}\left(r^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}+r^{p-\theta}(\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+1)\right],\end{array}

for some
\displaystyle C\equiv C(n,p,q,s,\Lambda)
. Here
\displaystyle\sigma
is given by [
24
, Lemma 4.2].
Eq.

4.19
now holds with
\displaystyle\sigma_{0}=\operatorname{min}\{\sigma,\bar{\alpha},p\bar{\alpha}/2\}.

∎

We are now ready to prove
Theorem

2.19
.

Proof of
Theorem

2.19

(a) For
\displaystyle 2\leq q\leq p
, we have already proved
Lemma

4.1
which shows the same decay estimate as of [
24
, Lemma 3.1] holds for our
\displaystyle\operatorname{snail}
term. Also
Eqs.

4.12
and
4.19
implies that the same Caccioppoli inequality and difference estimate holds like [
24
, lemma 4.1, Lemma 4.2] for the local solutions involving our
\displaystyle\operatorname{snail}
term. Further, regularity estimates for
\displaystyle h
have been obtained in
Lemma

4.2
. One can thus follow [
24
, Subsection 4.3, Pages 30-34] and end up with ([
24
, Equation (4.40)])

{}\begin{array}[]{l}\left[\fint_{B_{r}}|u-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{p}dx+r^{\delta}\left(r^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}+r^{p-\theta}(\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+1)\right]\\
\leq C\left(\frac{r}{\tilde{r}}\right)^{p\eta}\left[\fint_{B_{\tilde{r}}}|u-(u)_{B_{\tilde{r}}}|^{p}dx+\tilde{r}^{\delta}\left(\tilde{r}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\tilde{r}}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{\tilde{r}}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}+\tilde{r}^{p\eta}(\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{\tilde{r}})}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}+1)\right]\end{array}

(4.27)

for each
\displaystyle\eta\in(0,1)
and for all
\displaystyle 0<r\leq\tilde{r}\leq r_{*}\equiv r_{*}(n,p,q,s,\bar{\alpha},\Lambda,\eta)
, where
\displaystyle C\equiv C(n,p,q,s,\Lambda,\eta)
. Further estimating

{}\begin{array}[]{l}\quad\fint_{B_{r_{*}}}|u-(u)_{B_{r_{*}}}|^{p}dx+{r_{*}}^{\delta}\left({r_{*}}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r_{*}}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r_{*}}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\\
\leq\frac{C}{{r_{*}}^{n}}\|u\|^{p}_{L^{p}(B_{r_{*}})}+Cr_{*}^{\delta}\left(r_{*}^{-s{(q-1)}}\operatorname{Tail}_{q-1}(u;x_{0},r_{*})^{q-1}+r_{*}^{-s{(q-1)}}\left(\fint_{B_{r_{*}}}|u|\right)^{q-1}\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\\
\leq\frac{C}{{r_{*}}^{n}}\|u\|^{p}_{L^{p}(B_{r_{*}})}+Cr_{*}^{\delta-sq}r_{*}^{-nq/p}\left(\operatorname{Tail}_{q-1}(u;x_{0},r_{*})^{q}+\|u\|^{q}_{L^{p}(B_{r_{*}})}\right),\end{array}

(4.28)

holds for some
\displaystyle C\equiv C(n,p,q,s)
. Therefore, in view of
Eq.

4.27
, we have proved that for any
\displaystyle\eta\in(0,1)
,
\displaystyle\exists\,r_{*}\in(0,1)
depending on
\displaystyle n,p,q,s,\bar{\alpha},\Lambda\text{ and }\eta
such that

{}\fint_{B_{r}}|u-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{p}dx\leq C\left(\frac{r}{{r_{*}}}\right)^{p\eta}

(4.29)

holds with

{}C\equiv C(n,s,p,q,\bar{\alpha},\Lambda,\eta,\|u\|_{L^{p}(B_{{r}_{*}})},\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{{r}_{*}})},Tail_{q-1}(u;x_{0},r_{*}))\equiv C(\operatorname{data}_{h}(\eta),\eta)\geq 1

(4.30)

whenever
\displaystyle B_{r}\subset\subset\Omega
and
\displaystyle r\leq r_{*}
.
Eqs.

4.29
and
4.30
proves the first part of
Theorem

2.19
along with the desired estimate via the classical Campanato-Meyers integral characterization of Hölder continuity.

(b) For this part, we note [
24
, Remark 3, Remark 7], i.e. their set-up allows solutions to be in
\displaystyle W^{1,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
with the property that
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|u(x)|^{q}}{(1+|x|^{n+sq})}dx<\infty
(this is easy to check from [
24
, Equation 4.40]).
Since
\displaystyle u\geq 0
is bounded and for some
\displaystyle\theta\geq 1
,
\displaystyle u^{\theta}\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
i.e.
\displaystyle u^{\theta}\in L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, therefore for some
\displaystyle C>0
, it holds

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{u(x)^{q}}{(1+|x|^{n+sq})}dx\leq C\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{u(x)^{\theta q}}{(1+|x|^{n+sq})}dx\right)^{1/\theta}=C\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{u(x)^{\theta q}}{(1+|x|^{n+sq})}dx\right)^{1/\theta}<\infty.

Also,
Remark

3.2
and estimate of
\displaystyle\mathrm{(I)}
in
Lemma

4.3
implies we can take the local operator as
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{L}u(x)=\mathcal{L}_{L}^{A}u(x):=-\operatorname{div}A(x,\nabla u(x))
in the proofs of
[
24
]
. Hence, the local Hölder regularity of
\displaystyle u
follows from
[
24
]
.

5
Higher Hölder regularity results for local solutions

Using the almost Lipschitz regularity obtained in
Section

4
, we now show that gradients of local weak solutions of
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}u=f
in
\displaystyle\Omega
are also Hölder continuous, provided
\displaystyle f\in L^{d}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
for some
\displaystyle d>n
. In what follows, we take arbitrary open sets
\displaystyle\Omega_{0}\subset\subset\Omega_{1}\subset\subset\Omega
, and denote
\displaystyle\bar{d}:=\operatorname{min}\{\operatorname{dist}(\Omega_{0},\Omega_{1}),\operatorname{dist}(\Omega_{1},\Omega),1\}
. We consider
\displaystyle B_{r}\equiv B_{r}(x_{0})\subset\subset\Omega_{1}
with
\displaystyle x_{0}\in\Omega_{0}
and
\displaystyle 0<r\leq\bar{d}/4
and all the balls will be centred at
\displaystyle x_{0}
. Moreover,
\displaystyle\eta,\lambda
will be real numbers satisfying
\displaystyle\eta\in(s,1)
and
\displaystyle\lambda>0
; their precise values will be prescribed later depending on the context. We shall often use
Theorem

2.19
in the form
\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{0,\eta}(\Omega_{1})}\leq c\equiv c(\operatorname{data}(\eta),\eta,\bar{d})
, (
\displaystyle\operatorname{data}_{h}
is given in
Eq.

4.30
) for every
\displaystyle\eta<1
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Under the assumptions on
Theorem

2.20
and
\displaystyle 2\leq q\leq p<\infty
,

(a) If
\displaystyle s<\eta<1
, then there exists
\displaystyle c\equiv c(\operatorname{data}_{h}(\eta),\bar{d},\eta)>0
such that

{}\fint_{B_{r/2}}\int_{B_{r/2}}\frac{|u(x)-u(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\leq cr^{(\eta-s)q}.

(5.1)

(b) There exists
\displaystyle c\equiv c(\operatorname{data}_{h},\bar{d})>0
, such that whenever
\displaystyle 0<t\leq r
, it holds

{}t^{-\delta}[\operatorname{snail}(u,B_{t}(x_{0}))]^{q}=\left(t^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{t}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{t}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\leq c.

(5.2)

(c) If
\displaystyle\lambda>0
then there exists
\displaystyle c\equiv c(\operatorname{data}_{h}(\lambda),\bar{d},\lambda)>0
such that it holds

{}\fint_{B_{r/2}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx\leq cr^{-p\lambda}.

(5.3)

Proof.

Estimate
Eq.

5.1
follows from
Theorem

2.19
. To prove
Eq.

5.2
, we notice from the proof of
Lemma

4.1
(the part estimate of
\displaystyle T_{1}
), that

{}|(u)_{B_{\bar{d}}}-(u)_{B_{t}}|\leq c\int_{t}^{\bar{d}}\left(\fint_{B_{v}}|u(x)-(u)_{B_{v}}|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}\frac{dv}{v}+c\left(\fint_{B_{\bar{d}}}|u(x)-(u)_{B_{\bar{d}}}|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}\leq c\int_{t}^{\bar{d}}v^{\eta-1}dv+c\bar{d}^{\eta}\leq c\bar{d}^{\eta},

(5.4)

where
\displaystyle c\equiv c(\operatorname{data}_{h}(\eta),d,\eta)
. Using
Eq.

5.4
, we estimate as follows:

\begin{array}[]{l}t^{-\delta}[\operatorname{snail}(u,B_{t}(x_{0}))]^{q}\leq C\left(t^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\bar{d}}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{\bar{d}}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}+C\left(t^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\bar{d}}}\frac{|(u)_{B_{\bar{d}}}-(u)_{B_{t}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\left(t^{s}\int_{B_{\bar{d}}\backslash B_{t}}\frac{|u(y)-u(x_{0})|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}+C\left(t^{s}\int_{B_{\bar{d}}\backslash B_{t}}\frac{|u(x_{0})-(u)_{B_{t}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{estisnail}}}{{\leq}}C(\bar{d}^{-sq}\operatorname{Tail}_{q-1}(u;x_{0},\bar{d})^{q}+\bar{d}^{-n-sq}\|u\|_{L^{q}(\Omega_{1})})+C(t^{s}\bar{d}^{-sq}|(u)_{B_{\bar{d}}}-(u)_{B_{t}}|^{q-1})^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\left(\int_{B_{\bar{d}}\backslash B_{t}}\frac{|y-x_{0}|^{\eta(q-1)}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+s(q-1)}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}+C\left(t^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{t}}\frac{t^{\eta(q-1)}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\leq C\bar{d}^{-n-sq}+C(\bar{d}^{-s(q-1)}\bar{d}^{\eta(q-1)})^{\frac{q}{q-1}}+C(\bar{d}^{(\eta-s)(q-1)})^{\frac{q}{q-1}}+C(t^{(\eta-s)(q-1)})^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\leq C\bar{d}^{-n-sq}+C\bar{d}^{(\eta-s)q}+Ct^{(\eta-s)q}\leq C,\end{array}

where
\displaystyle C\equiv C(\operatorname{data}_{h},\bar{d},\eta)
, which gives
Eq.

5.2
if we choose
\displaystyle\eta=(1+s)/2
. Finally, to prove
Eq.

5.3
, we use
Eq.

4.12
and estimate the various terms as

r^{-p}[\operatorname{av}_{p}(r)]^{p}\leq cr^{p(\eta-1)},

holds with
\displaystyle c\equiv c(\operatorname{data}_{h},\bar{d},\eta)
, by
Theorem

2.19
. By
Eq.

5.2
we instead have

r^{-\delta}[\operatorname{snail}(r)]^{q}+\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r})}^{p/(p-1)}+1\leq c\leq cr^{p(\eta-1)}.

Choosing
\displaystyle\eta
such that
\displaystyle 1-\eta\leq\lambda
, we arrive at
Eq.

5.3
.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\displaystyle 2\leq q\leq p<\infty
. If
\displaystyle h\in u+W_{0}^{1,p}(B_{r/4}(x_{0}))
the solution of
Eq.

4.13
, then

{}\fint_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}|\nabla u-\nabla h|^{p}dx\leq cr^{\sigma_{2}p}

(5.5)

holds where
\displaystyle\sigma_{2}\equiv\sigma_{2}(n,p,s,q,\bar{d})\in(0,1)
and
\displaystyle c\equiv c(\operatorname{data}_{h},\bar{d},\|f\|_{L^{d}(\Omega_{1})})
.

Proof.

We go back to
Lemma

4.3
, estimate
Eq.

4.22
, and adopting the notation introduced there, we improve the estimates for the terms
\displaystyle\mathrm{(I)}-\mathrm{(IV)}
. As in
Eq.

4.23
, we find using
Eq.

5.3

{}\begin{array}[]{l}|(\mathrm{I})|\leq Cr^{\bar{\alpha}}\left(\fint_{B_{r/4}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}dx\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\fint_{B_{r/4}}|\nabla u-\nabla h|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{h1}}}{{\leq}}C_{\lambda}r^{\bar{\alpha}-p\lambda},\end{array}

(5.6)

for some
\displaystyle C_{\lambda}>0
. Next, we estimate by Sobolev inequality

{}\begin{array}[]{rcl}|(\mathrm{II})|{\leq}C\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r/4})}\left(\fint_{B_{r/4}}|\nabla u-\nabla h|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{h1}}}{{\leq}}&C\|f\|_{L^{n}(B_{r/4})}\left(\fint_{B_{r/4}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx\right)^{1/p}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{higherholder3}}}{{\leq}}&C_{\lambda}\|f\|_{L^{d}(B_{r/4})}r^{1-n/d-\lambda},\end{array}

(5.7)

for some
\displaystyle C_{\lambda}>0
. Now we use Hölder inequality and
Lemma

2.5
and note that
\displaystyle u-h\equiv 0
outside
\displaystyle B_{r/4}(x_{0})
, to estimate
\displaystyle\mathrm{(III)}
as

{}\begin{array}[]{rcl}|(\mathrm{III})|&\leq&C\left(\fint_{B_{r/2}}\int_{B_{r/2}}\frac{|u(x)-u(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\left(\fint_{B_{r/2}}\int_{B_{r/2}}\frac{|(u-h)(x)-(u-h)(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{higherhol1}}}{{\leq}}&Cr^{(\eta-s)(q-1)}r^{1-s}\left(\fint_{B_{r/4}}|\nabla u-\nabla h|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{h1}}}{{\leq}}&Cr^{(\eta-s)(q-1)}r^{1-s}\left(\fint_{B_{r/4}}(|\nabla u|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx\right)^{1/p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{higherholder3}}}{{\leq}}C_{\lambda}r^{(\eta-s)(q-1)+1-s-\lambda},\end{array}

(5.8)

whenever
\displaystyle s<\eta<1
and
\displaystyle\lambda>0
. As
\displaystyle u
is locally Hölder continuous, by Maz’ya-Wiener boundary regularity theory,
\displaystyle h
is continuous on
\displaystyle\bar{B}_{r/4}
and therefore

{}\|u-h\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r/4})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{h2}}}{{\leq}}2\underset{\partial B_{r/4}}{\operatorname{osc}}\,u\leq 4[u]_{C^{0,\eta}(B_{r/4})}r^{\eta}\leq Cr^{\eta},

(5.9)

where
\displaystyle C\equiv C(\operatorname{data}_{h},\bar{d},\eta)
. To estimate
\displaystyle\mathrm{(IV)}
we restart from the third line of
Eq.

4.24
, and using also
Eqs.

5.2
and
5.9
, we easily find

{}\begin{array}[]{l}|(\mathrm{IV})|\leq C\left[r^{-sq}\left(\fint_{B_{r/2}}|u(x)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q}\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r/2}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{r/2}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right]\left(\fint_{B_{r/2}}|u(x)-h(x)|^{q}dx\right)^{1/q}\\
\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{higherholder2}}}{{\leq}}(r^{\eta(q-1)-sq}+r^{-s})\|u-h\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r/4})}\leq C(r^{\eta(q-1)-sq}+r^{-s})r^{\eta}\leq C_{\eta}r^{\eta-s}.\end{array}

(5.10)

Merging
Eqs.

5.6
,
5.7
,
5.8
and
5.10
, we get from
Eq.

4.22

{}\fint_{B_{r/4}}|V_{\mu}(\nabla u)-V_{\mu}(\nabla h)|^{2}dx\leq C_{\eta,\lambda}(r^{\bar{\alpha}-p\lambda}+r^{1-n/d-\lambda}+r^{(\eta-s)(q-1)-\lambda}+r^{\eta-s}),

(5.11)

for every
\displaystyle\eta\in(s,1)
and
\displaystyle\lambda>0
. We then choose
\displaystyle\eta,\lambda
such that

\eta:=\frac{1+s}{2},\quad 0<\lambda=\min\left\{\frac{\bar{\alpha}}{2p},\frac{(1-s)(q-1)}{4},\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{n}{d}\right)\right\}

and put in
Eq.

5.11
to get

{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\fint_{B_{r/4}}|V_{\mu}(\nabla u)-V_{\mu}(\nabla h)|^{2}dx\leq Cr^{\sigma_{1}p}\\
\sigma_{1}:=\frac{1}{p}\min\left\{\frac{\bar{\alpha}}{2},\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{n}{d}\right),\frac{(1-s)(q-1)}{4},\frac{1-s}{2}\right\}>0\end{array}\right.

(5.12)

for
\displaystyle C\equiv C(\operatorname{data}_{h},\bar{d},\|f\|_{L^{d}(\Omega_{1})})
. By Hölder inequality,
Eqs.

5.12
and
4.20
gives
Eq.

5.5
with
\displaystyle\sigma_{2}=\sigma_{1}
as
\displaystyle p\geq 2
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

2.20

(a) Once
Eq.

5.5
is established, we can conclude the local Hölder continuity of
\displaystyle\nabla u
by a classical comparison argument (see for instance
[
44
]
). We briefly include it here for the sake of completeness. For every
\displaystyle 0<t\leq r/8
, we estimate using
Eq.

4.2

\begin{array}[]{l}\fint_{B_{t}}|\nabla u-(\nabla u)_{B_{t}}|^{p}dx\leq c(\underset{B_{t}}{\operatorname{osc}}\,\nabla h)^{p}+c\left(\frac{r}{t}\right)^{n}\fint_{B_{r/4}}|\nabla u-\nabla h|^{p}dx\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{h3},\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{higherholder4}}}{{\leq}}c\left(\frac{t}{r}\right)^{\alpha_{0}p}\fint_{B_{r/4}}(|\nabla h|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx+c\left(\frac{r}{t}\right)^{n}r^{\sigma_{2}p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{h1},\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{higherholder3}}}{{\leq}}c\left(\frac{t}{r}\right)^{\alpha_{0}p}r^{-\lambda p}+c\left(\frac{r}{t}\right)^{n}r^{\sigma_{2}p},\end{array}

with
\displaystyle c\equiv c(\operatorname{data}_{h},\bar{d},\lambda,\|f\|_{L^{d}(\Omega_{1})})
. In the above inequality, we take
\displaystyle t=\frac{1}{8}r^{\frac{2n+\sigma_{2}p}{2n}}
and choose
\displaystyle\lambda:=\frac{\sigma_{2}p\alpha_{0}}{4n}
in
Eq.

5.3
. We conclude with

\fint_{B_{t}}|\nabla u-(\nabla u)_{B_{t}}|^{p}dx\leq ct^{\bar{\eta}p},\quad\bar{\eta}:=\min\left\{\frac{n{\sigma}_{2}}{2n+{\sigma}_{2}p},\frac{\sigma_{2}\alpha_{0}p}{2\sigma_{2}p+4n}\right\}

where
\displaystyle c\equiv c(\operatorname{data}_{h},\bar{d},\|f\|_{L^{d}(\Omega_{1})})
. It holds whenever
\displaystyle B_{t}\subset\subset\Omega
is a ball centred in
\displaystyle\Omega_{0}
, with
\displaystyle t\leq\bar{d}^{1+\sigma_{2}p/(2n)}/c(n,p)
. As
\displaystyle\Omega_{0}\subset\subset\Omega_{1}\subset\subset\Omega
are arbitrary, this implies the local
\displaystyle C^{1,\bar{\eta}}
-regularity of
\displaystyle u
in
\displaystyle\Omega
, via the classical Campanato-Meyers’ integral characterization of Hölder continuity together with the estimate for
\displaystyle[\nabla u]_{0,\bar{\eta};\Omega_{0}}
, and the proof is complete.

(b) Note that (as already described in the proof of
Theorem

2.19
, part (b))
\displaystyle u
is positive and
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{u(x)^{q}}{(1+|x|^{n+sq})}dx<\infty
implies
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r}}\frac{u(x)^{q}}{|x-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dx<\infty,\forall B_{r}(x_{0})\subset\subset\Omega
and hence
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r}}\frac{|u(x)-(u)_{B_{r}}|^{q}}{|x-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}dx<\infty,\forall B_{r}(x_{0})\subset\subset\Omega
. Thus lemma 6.1 of
[
24
]
follows. In view of the proof of
Lemma

5.2
, estimate of
\displaystyle\mathrm{(}I)
and choice of
\displaystyle\lambda,\sigma_{1}
it is clear that [
24
, Lemma 6.2] holds for the choice of operator
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}
, with
\displaystyle\sigma_{1}
given by
Eq.

5.12
. Therefore the gradient regularity follows from
[
24
]
.

6
Up to the boundary Hölder regularity

We now proceed with the proof of
Theorem

2.21
. The local
\displaystyle C^{1,\gamma}
(for some
\displaystyle\gamma\in(0,1)
) regularity follows from
[
24
]
. We show the boundary regularity under the conditions of
Theorem

2.21
. To do this, it is convenient to locally straighten the boundary around any given point
\displaystyle x_{0}\in\partial\Omega
. Without loss of generality (by translation) we can assume that
\displaystyle x_{0}\in\{x_{n}=0\}
and that
\displaystyle\Omega
touches
\displaystyle\{x_{n}=0\}
tangentially, so that its normal at
\displaystyle x_{0}
is
\displaystyle e_{n}
, where
\displaystyle\{e_{i}\}_{i\leq n}
is the standard basis of
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{n}
. As
\displaystyle\partial\Omega
is
\displaystyle C^{2}
, following the argument of [
24
, Section 5] (and mostly adopting its notation), we see that there exists a global
\displaystyle C^{1,\bar{\alpha}}
-diffeomorphism
\displaystyle\mathcal{T}
of
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\mathcal{T}(x_{0})=x_{0},\quad B_{2r_{0}}^{+}(x_{0})\subset\mathcal{T}(\Omega_{3r_{0}}(x_{0}))\subset B_{4r_{0}}^{+}(x_{0}),\quad\Gamma_{2r_{0}}(x_{0})\subset\mathcal{T}(\partial\Omega\cap B_{3r_{0}}(x_{0}))\subset\Gamma_{4r_{0}}(x_{0}),

for some small radius
\displaystyle r_{0}\in(0,1]
. Here
\displaystyle B_{r_{0}}^{+}(x_{0})=B_{r}(x_{0})\cap\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:x_{n}>0\},\,\Omega_{r}(x_{0})=\Omega\cap B_{r}(x_{0})
and
\displaystyle\Gamma_{r}(x_{0})=B_{r}(x_{0})\cap\{x_{n}=0\}
. We write
\displaystyle\mathcal{S}:=\mathcal{T}^{-1}
and
\displaystyle\mathbf{c}:=|\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{S}}|
, with
\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{S}}
denoting the Jacobian determinant of the inverse
\displaystyle\mathcal{S}
. Let
\displaystyle\widetilde{\Omega}:=\mathcal{T}(\Omega),\tilde{g}:=g\circ\mathcal{S},\tilde{f}:=\mathbf{c}(f\circ\mathcal{S})
, and
\displaystyle\tilde{u}:=u\circ\mathcal{S}.
The diffeomorphism
\displaystyle\mathcal{T}
leaves all the structural conditions on
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}
unchanged and the same is true for
\displaystyle f
and
\displaystyle u
given in
Theorem

2.21
. Moreover,
\displaystyle\tilde{u}\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\widetilde{\Omega})
is a weak solution of

{}-\operatorname{div}\tilde{A}(\cdot,\nabla\tilde{u})+\widetilde{Q}_{N}\tilde{u}=\tilde{f}\text{ in }\widetilde{\Omega};\quad\tilde{u}=0\text{ in }\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\widetilde{\Omega},

(6.1)

where

\widetilde{A}(x,z):=\mathbf{c}(x)A(\mathcal{S}(x),z(D\mathcal{T}\circ\mathcal{S})(x))(D\mathcal{T}\circ\mathcal{S})(x)^{T}

and

\widetilde{Q}_{N}u(x):=\text{ P.V. }\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi(u(x)-u(y))\widetilde{K}(x,y)dy,

with

\widetilde{K}(x,y):=\mathbf{c}(x)\mathbf{c}(y)\frac{B(\mathcal{S}(x),\mathcal{S}(y))}{|\mathcal{S}(x)-\mathcal{S}(y)|^{n+sq}}.

From assumptions
Eqs.

2.6
,
2.7
and
2.8
and the regularity of
\displaystyle\mathcal{T}
, we infer that

{}\begin{cases}0<\widetilde{\Lambda}^{-1}\leq\mathbf{c}(x)\leq\widetilde{\Lambda}&\text{ for all }x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\\
|\mathbf{c}(x)-\mathbf{c}(y)|\leq\widetilde{\Lambda}|x-y|^{\bar{\alpha}}&\text{ for all }x,y\in B_{r_{0}}(x_{0}),\\
\widetilde{K}(x,y)=\widetilde{K}(y,x)&\text{ for a.e. }x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\\
\frac{\widetilde{\Lambda}^{-1}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}\leq\widetilde{K}(x,y)\leq\frac{\widetilde{\Lambda}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}&\text{ for a.e. }x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\end{cases}

(6.2)

and that the
\displaystyle p
-growth and coercivity conditions are preserved, namely

{}\begin{cases}|\widetilde{A}(x,z)|+|z||\partial_{z}\widetilde{A}(x,z)|\leq\widetilde{\Lambda}(|z|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|z|&\text{ for all }x\in B_{r_{0}}^{+}(x_{0}),\\
|\widetilde{A}(x,z)-\widetilde{A}(y,z)|\leq\widetilde{\Lambda}(|z|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-1}{2}}|x-y|^{\bar{\alpha}}&\text{ for all }x,y\in B_{r_{0}}^{+}(x_{0})\\
\langle\partial_{z}\widetilde{A}(x,z)\xi,\xi\rangle\geq\widetilde{\Lambda}^{-1}(|z|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|\xi|^{2}&\text{ for all }x\in B_{r_{0}}^{+}(x_{0}),\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\end{cases}

(6.3)

for every
\displaystyle z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\{0\}
and for some constant
\displaystyle\widetilde{\Lambda}\geq 1
depending only on
\displaystyle n,p,\bar{\alpha},\Lambda
, and
\displaystyle\Omega
.
The conditions on
\displaystyle f
and
\displaystyle u
in
Theorem

2.21
now reads as

{}0\leq\tilde{f}(x)\leq cx_{n}^{-\sigma},\quad 0\leq\tilde{u}(x)\leq M_{\epsilon}x_{n}^{\epsilon},\,\quad\text{ for some }\epsilon\in[\sigma,1),\quad\text{ for all }x\in B_{r_{0}}^{+}(x_{0}).

(6.4)

We take any point
\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{0}\in\Gamma_{r_{0}/2}(x_{0})
, radius
\displaystyle\varrho\in\left(0,\frac{r_{0}}{4}\right]
, and upper balls
\displaystyle B_{\varrho}\equiv B^{+}_{\varrho}(\tilde{x}_{0})\subset B^{+}_{r_{0}}({x}_{0})
. Unless otherwise stated, all the upper balls will be centred at
\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{0}
, and
\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{0}
will be a fixed but generic point as specified.

Lemma 6.1
.

(Boundary Caccioppoli type inequality)
Under the assumptions of
Theorem

2.21
, the inequality

{}\begin{array}[]{l}\quad\fint_{B_{\varrho/2}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}(|\nabla\tilde{u}|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx+\fint_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\int_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{u}(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\\
\leq c\left(\varrho^{-p}\fint_{B_{\varrho}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\tilde{u}|^{p}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\varrho}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(y)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{\varrho}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q}}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy+1\right)\end{array}

(6.5)

holds with
\displaystyle c\equiv c(n,p,s,q,\Lambda,\sigma,\Omega)
.

Proof.

Let us fix parameters
\displaystyle\varrho/2\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\leq\varrho
, a function
\displaystyle\eta\in C_{0}^{1}(B_{\tau_{2}})
such that
\displaystyle\chi_{B_{\tau_{1}}}\leq\eta\leq\chi_{B_{(3\tau_{2}+\tau_{1})/4}}
and
\displaystyle|\nabla\eta|\lesssim 1/(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})
. Consider
\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}:=\eta^{p}\tilde{u}
. By definition,
\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}
vanishes outside
\displaystyle B_{\tau_{2}}^{+}\subset B_{\varrho}^{+}\subset B_{r_{0}}^{+}(x_{0})
, so that
\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}\in W_{0}^{1,p}(B_{\varrho}^{+})
. Taking
\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}
as a test function and noting [
24
, Section 2.5], we find

\begin{array}[]{rcl}0&=&\int_{B_{\varrho}^{+}}\eta^{p}\left[\tilde{A}(x,\nabla\tilde{u})\cdot\nabla\tilde{u}-\tilde{f}\tilde{u}\right]dx+p\int_{B_{\varrho}^{+}}\tilde{u}\,\eta^{p-1}\tilde{A}(x,\nabla\tilde{u})\cdot\nabla\eta\,dx\\
&&+\int_{B_{\tau_{2}}}\int_{B_{\tau_{2}}}|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{u}(y)|^{q-2}(\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{u}(y))\cdot(\eta^{p}(x)\tilde{u}(x)-\eta^{p}(y)\tilde{u}(y))\tilde{K}(x,y)dxdy\\
&&+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\tau_{2}}}\int_{B_{\tau_{2}}}|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{u}(y)|^{q-2}(\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{u}(y))\eta^{p}(x)\tilde{u}(x)\tilde{K}(x,y)dxdy\\
&=:&(\mathrm{I})+(\mathrm{II})+(\mathrm{IIII})+(\mathrm{IV}).\end{array}

In view of [
24
, Lemma 5.1], it is enough to estimate the term involving
\displaystyle\tilde{f}
only. As
\displaystyle 0<\sigma<1
,
\displaystyle 0\leq\eta\leq 1
and
\displaystyle\varrho<1
, we have by
Eqs.

6.2
and
6.4

\int_{B_{\varrho}^{+}}\eta^{p}\tilde{f}\tilde{u}\,dx\leq c\int_{B_{\varrho}^{+}}x_{n}^{\epsilon-\sigma}dx\leq c\varrho^{n+\epsilon-\sigma}\leq c,

for some
\displaystyle c\equiv c(\sigma,n)
. Proof of
Eq.

6.5
now follows from [
24
, Lemma 5.1] (taking
\displaystyle\tilde{g}\equiv 0
there).
∎

Consider now the boundary
\displaystyle p
-harmonic function
\displaystyle\tilde{h}\in W_{\tilde{u}}^{1,p}(B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0}))
of the homogeneous Dirichlet problem

{}\begin{cases}-\operatorname{div}\widetilde{A}(\tilde{x}_{0},\nabla\tilde{h})=0&\text{ in }B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0}),\\
\tilde{h}=\tilde{u}&\text{ on }\partial B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})\end{cases}

(6.6)

In the next lemma, which is a boundary version of
Lemma

4.2
, we collect some useful properties of
\displaystyle\tilde{h}
, which are essentially all contained in [
41
, Lemma 5]. The existence and uniqueness of
\displaystyle\tilde{h}
is classical and is mentioned, for instance, in
[
41
]
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
\displaystyle\tilde{h}
be the solution of problem
Eq.

6.6
. Then, there exist constants
\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}_{0}\in(0,1)
and
\displaystyle C>0
such that,

{}\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}(|\nabla\tilde{h}|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx\leq C\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}(|\nabla\tilde{u}|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx,

(6.7)

{}\|\tilde{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0}))}\leq\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0}))},\quad\underset{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}{\operatorname{osc}}\tilde{h}\leq\underset{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}{\operatorname{osc}}\tilde{u},

(6.8)

{}\fint_{B_{t}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}(|\nabla\tilde{h}|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx\leq c\left(\frac{t}{\varrho}\right)^{-n+b}\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}(|\nabla\tilde{h}|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx

(6.9)

and

{}\underset{B^{+}_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}{\operatorname{osc}}\nabla\tilde{h}\leq c\left(\frac{t}{\varrho}\right)^{\tilde{\alpha}_{0}}\left(\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}(|\nabla\tilde{h}|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx\right)^{1/p}

(6.10)

holds for all
\displaystyle t\in(0,\varrho/8]
with
\displaystyle c\equiv c(n,p,q,s,\Lambda)
and
\displaystyle b
such that
\displaystyle 0\leq b<n
.

Proof.

The proof of
Eq.

6.7
follows similarly to the proof of
Eq.

4.15
. The proof of
Eq.

6.8
follows immediately from the weak maximum principle. Proof of
Eq.

6.9
is given in [
24
, section 5.7] and finally the proof of
Eq.

6.10
is established in [
41
, lemma 5].
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Let the conditions of
Theorem

2.21
are satisfied and
\displaystyle\tilde{h}\in W_{\tilde{u}}^{1,p}(B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0}))
be the solution of
Eq.

6.6
. There exists
\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}_{0}\equiv\tilde{\sigma}_{0}(p,s,q,\bar{\alpha})\in

\displaystyle(0,1)
such that

{}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\tilde{u}-\tilde{h}|^{p}dx\leq c\varrho^{\theta\tilde{\sigma}_{0}}\left[\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\tilde{u}|^{p}dx+\varrho^{\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\varrho}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(y)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{\varrho}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q}}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy+\varrho^{p-\theta}\right],

(6.11)

holds for every
\displaystyle\theta\in(0,1)
where
\displaystyle\delta\in(sq,p)
,
\displaystyle c\equiv c(n,p,q,s,\sigma,\Lambda)
.

Proof.

Similarly like
Lemma

4.3
, noting [
24
, Section 2.5], we take the test function
\displaystyle\tilde{u}-\tilde{h}\in W_{0}^{1,p}(B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(x_{0}))
in the equations of
\displaystyle\tilde{u}
and
\displaystyle\tilde{h}
and subtract to get

{}\begin{array}[]{rcl}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}}|V_{\mu}(\nabla\tilde{u})-V_{\mu}(\nabla\tilde{h})|^{2}dx&\leq&c\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}}(\tilde{A}(\tilde{x}_{0},\nabla\tilde{u})-\tilde{A}(\tilde{x}_{0},\nabla\tilde{h}))\cdot(\nabla\tilde{u}-\nabla\tilde{h})dx\\
&=&c\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}}\tilde{A}(\tilde{x}_{0},\nabla\tilde{u})\cdot(\nabla\tilde{u}-\nabla\tilde{h})dx\\
&=&c\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}}(\tilde{A}(\tilde{x}_{0},\nabla\tilde{u})-\tilde{A}(x,\nabla\tilde{u}))\cdot(\nabla\tilde{u}-\nabla\tilde{h})dx+c\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}}\tilde{f}(\tilde{u}-\tilde{h})dx\\
&&-\frac{c}{|B_{\varrho}^{+}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{u}(y)|^{q-2}(v)((\tilde{u}-\tilde{h})(x)-(\tilde{u}-\tilde{h})(y))\tilde{K}(x,y)dxdy\\
&=&(\mathrm{I})+(\mathrm{II})+(\mathrm{III}),\end{array}

(6.12)

where
\displaystyle c\equiv c(n,p,\tilde{\Lambda})
. In view of [
24
, Lemma 5.2] and our growth conditions
Eqs.

6.2
and
6.3
, we only estimate the second term. As
\displaystyle 0<\sigma<1
, we have using
Eq.

6.4
and
Eq.

6.8

{}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}}|\tilde{f}(\tilde{u}-\tilde{h})|dx\leq 2\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}}|\tilde{f}|\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{+}_{\varrho/4})}dx\leq c\varrho^{\epsilon}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}}x_{n}^{-\sigma}dx\leq c\varrho^{\epsilon-\sigma}\leq c,

for some
\displaystyle c\equiv c(n,\sigma)
. Proof of
Eq.

6.11
now follows from [
24
, Lemma 5.2].
∎

Once
Lemma

6.1
and
Lemma

6.3
are established, then using the growth conditions
Eq.

6.2
,
Eq.

6.3
and
Lemma

6.2
, one can follow [
24
, Section 5.6], to establish the following global Hölder regularity of solutions
\displaystyle u
satisfying conditions of
Theorem

2.21
.

Theorem 6.4
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

2.21
,
\displaystyle u\in C^{0,\eta}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for all
\displaystyle\eta\in(0,1)
and
\displaystyle[u]_{0,\eta,\mathbb{R}^{n}}\leq c(\operatorname{data})\equiv c(n,p,s,\eta,q,\Lambda,\sigma,\Omega)
.

To prove
Theorem

2.21
, we proceed with the ideas of [
24
, Section 6] and prove the following lemmas.

Lemma 6.5
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

2.21
,
\displaystyle\tilde{u}
(solution of
Eq.

6.1
) satisfies

{}\fint_{B_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\int_{B_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{u}(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\leq C_{\eta}t^{(\eta-s)q}\quad\text{ for all }\eta\in(s,1)\text{ and }t\in\left(0,\frac{r_{0}}{4}\right)\text{, }

(6.13)

{}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(y)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q}}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\leq C\quad\text{ for all }t\in\left(0,\frac{r_{0}}{4}\right),

(6.14)

{}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}(|\nabla\tilde{u}|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx\leq C_{\lambda}\varrho^{-\lambda p}\quad\text{ for all }\lambda>0\text{ and }\varrho\in\left(0,\frac{r_{0}}{4}\right)\text{. }

(6.15)

The constant
\displaystyle C_{\eta}
depends on
\displaystyle\eta
, while
\displaystyle C_{\lambda}
depends on
\displaystyle\lambda
.

Proof.

Eq.

6.13
follows from
Theorem

6.4
. To establish
Eq.

6.14
, we estimate as

\begin{array}[]{l}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(y)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q}}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{r_{0}}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(y)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q}}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy+2^{q-1}\int_{B_{r_{0}}(\tilde{x}_{0})\backslash B_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(y)-\tilde{u}(\tilde{x}_{0})|^{q}}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2^{q-1}|\tilde{u}(\tilde{x}_{0})-(\tilde{u})_{B_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q}\int_{B_{r_{0}}(\tilde{x}_{0})\backslash B_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{dy}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}\\
\leq C\left[r_{0}^{-sq}\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}+[\tilde{u}]_{C^{\eta}(B_{r_{0}}(\tilde{x}_{0}))}^{q}\left(\int_{B_{r_{0}}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{dy}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+(s-\eta)q}}+t^{\eta q}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{t}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{dy}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}\right)\right]\\
\leq C_{\eta}(r_{0}^{-sq}+r_{0}^{(\eta-s)q}+t^{(\eta-s)q}),\end{array}

for every
\displaystyle\eta\in(s,1)
and some constant
\displaystyle C_{\eta}>0
depending on
\displaystyle\eta
and
\displaystyle\operatorname{data}
as mentioned in
Theorem

6.4
. By choosing
\displaystyle\beta=(1+s)/2
, we find the desired inequality
Eq.

6.14
.
Finally, to prove
Eq.

6.15
, we recall the boundary Caccioppoli inequality
Lemma

6.1
. Given
Eq.

6.14
, we only need to estimate the first term on the right hand side of
Eq.

6.5
. Noting that
\displaystyle\tilde{u}(\tilde{x}_{0})=0
, we have

\varrho^{-p}\fint_{B_{\varrho}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\tilde{u}|^{p}dx=\varrho^{-p}\fint_{B_{\varrho}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{u}(\tilde{x}_{0})|^{p}dx\leq C\varrho^{(\eta-1)p},

for some
\displaystyle C>0
depending on the parameters mentioned in
Theorem

6.4
. This along with
Eqs.

6.5
and
6.14
gives

\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}(|\nabla\tilde{u}|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}dx\leq C\varrho^{(\eta-1)p},

as
\displaystyle\varrho<1
. Choosing
\displaystyle\eta
such that
\displaystyle 1-\eta\leq\lambda
, we get
Eq.

6.15
.
∎

Remark 6.6
.

In view of
Eq.

6.8
and
Theorem

6.4
, it holds

\|\tilde{u}-\tilde{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0}))}\leq\underset{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}{\operatorname{osc}}\tilde{h}+\underset{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}{\operatorname{osc}}\tilde{u}\leq 2\underset{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}{\operatorname{osc}}\tilde{u}\leq 2[\tilde{u}]_{C^{\eta}(B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0}))}\varrho^{\eta}\leq C_{\eta}\varrho^{\eta}

for all
\displaystyle\eta\in(0,1)

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
\displaystyle\tilde{u}
and
\displaystyle\tilde{h}
be solutions of
Eq.

6.1
and
Eq.

6.6
respectively. Then under the condition of
Theorem

2.21
, there exists constants
\displaystyle C>0
and
\displaystyle\bar{\sigma}\in(0,1)
such that

{}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\nabla\tilde{u}-\nabla\tilde{h}|^{p}dx\leq C\varrho^{\bar{\sigma}p}.

(6.16)

Proof.

First note that, by
Eq.

6.7
and
Eq.

6.15
, it holds

{}\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\nabla\tilde{u}-\nabla\tilde{h}|^{p}dx\leq C_{\lambda}\varrho^{-\lambda p},

(6.17)

for every
\displaystyle\lambda>0
and for some constant
\displaystyle C_{\lambda}>0
depending also on
\displaystyle\lambda
. Now we estimate different terms present in the right hand side of
Eq.

6.12
. The first term can be estimated like the homonym term in
Lemma

5.2
using
Eqs.

6.3
,
6.7
,
6.15
and
6.17
and yields

{}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}(\tilde{A}(\tilde{x}_{0},\nabla\tilde{u})-\tilde{A}(x,\nabla\tilde{u}))\cdot(\nabla\tilde{u}-\nabla\tilde{h})dx\leq C_{\lambda}\varrho^{\bar{\alpha}-\lambda p},

(6.18)

for every
\displaystyle\lambda>0
. For, the second term, we use
Eq.

6.4
and
Remark

6.6
to get

{}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\tilde{f}|(\tilde{u}-\tilde{h})|dx\leq C\|\tilde{u}-\tilde{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0}))}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\tilde{f}dx\leq C_{\tilde{\eta}}\varrho^{\tilde{\eta}}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}x_{n}^{-\sigma}dx\leq C_{\tilde{\eta},\sigma}\varrho^{\tilde{\eta}-\sigma},

(6.19)

for every
\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}\in(\sigma,1)
. the third term can be broken as
\displaystyle|\mathrm{(III)}|=J_{1}+J_{2}
where

J_{1}:=\fint_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\int_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{u}(y)|^{q-1}|(\tilde{u}-\tilde{h})(x)-(\tilde{u}-\tilde{h})(y)|}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy,

and

J_{2}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\left(\fint_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{u}(y)|^{q-1}|(\tilde{u}-\tilde{h})(x)|}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dx\right)dy.

The term
\displaystyle J_{1}
can be estimated as the term
\displaystyle\mathrm{(III)}
in
Lemma

5.2
using
Lemma

2.5
,
Eq.

6.13
,
Eq.

6.17
and yields

{}J_{1}\leq C_{\eta,\lambda}\varrho^{(\eta-s)(q-1)-\lambda},

(6.20)

for every
\displaystyle\eta\in(s,1)
and
\displaystyle\lambda>0
.
Finally, we estimate
\displaystyle J_{2}
. Since
\displaystyle\tilde{u}-\tilde{h}
is supported in
\displaystyle B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})
and it holds

\displaystyle{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|}/{|x-y|}\leq 2\text{ for every }x\in B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})\text{ and }y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})

Therefore, we have

{}\begin{array}[]{rcl}J_{2}&\leq&C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\left(\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{\left(|\tilde{u}(x)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q-1}+|\tilde{u}(y)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q-1}\right)|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{h}(x)|}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}dx\right)dy\\
&\leq&C\varrho^{-sq}\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\tilde{u}(x)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q-1}|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{h}(x)|dx\\
&&+C\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(y)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q-1}}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)\left(\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{h}(x)|dx\right).\end{array}

(6.21)

By Hölder’s inequality,
Theorem

6.4
and
Remark

6.6
we obtain

\begin{array}[]{l}\quad\varrho^{-sq}\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\tilde{u}(x)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q-1}|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{h}(x)|dx\\
\leq C\varrho^{-sq}\left(\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\tilde{u}(x)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q}\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\left(\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{h}(x)|^{q}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\\
\leq C\varrho^{-sq+\eta(q-1)}[\tilde{u}]_{C^{\eta}(B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0}))}^{q-1}\|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{h}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0}))}\leq C_{\eta}\varrho^{(\eta-s)q},\end{array}

whereas, by Hölder’s inequality,
Lemma

2.6
,
Eq.

6.14
and
Remark

6.6
, we get

\begin{array}[]{l}\quad\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(y)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q-1}}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}dy\right)\left(\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{h}(x)|dx\right)\\
\leq C\varrho^{-s}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}\frac{|\tilde{u}(y)-(\tilde{u})_{B_{\varrho/2}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{q}}{|y-\tilde{x}_{0}|^{n+sq}}dydy\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\|\tilde{u}(x)-\tilde{h}(x)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0}))}\leq C_{\eta}\varrho^{\eta-s}.\end{array}

Merging the estimates in the last two displays and using in
Eq.

6.21
, we get

{}J_{2}\leq C_{\eta}\varrho^{\eta-s},

(6.22)

for every
\displaystyle\eta\in(s,1)
. We now merge
Eqs.

6.18
,
6.19
,
6.20
and
6.22
to get from
Eq.

6.12

{}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|V_{\mu}(\nabla\tilde{u})-V_{\mu}(\nabla\tilde{h})|^{2}dx\leq C_{\eta,\tilde{\eta},\lambda,\sigma}(\varrho^{\bar{\alpha}-\lambda p}+\varrho^{\tilde{\eta}-\sigma}+\varrho^{(\eta-s)(q-1)-\lambda}+\varrho^{\eta-s}),

(6.23)

for every
\displaystyle\eta\in(s,1)
,
\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}\in(\sigma,1)
and
\displaystyle\lambda>0
. We now choose the constants
\displaystyle\lambda
,
\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}
and
\displaystyle\eta
as follows:

\eta:=\frac{1+s}{2},\quad\tilde{\eta}=\frac{1+\sigma}{2}\quad\text{ and }\quad\lambda:=\min\left\{\frac{\bar{\alpha}}{2p},\frac{(1-s)(q-1)}{4}\right\},

so that
Eq.

6.23
becomes just

{}\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|V_{\mu}(\nabla\tilde{u})-V_{\mu}(\nabla\tilde{h})|^{2}dx\leq C_{\sigma}\varrho^{\sigma_{0}p},

(6.24)

with
\displaystyle\sigma_{0}:=\frac{1}{p}\min\left\{\frac{\bar{\alpha}}{2},\frac{(1-s)(q-1)}{4},\frac{1-s}{2},\frac{1-\sigma}{2}\right\}
.
We are now in position to conclude, using
Eq.

6.24
in combination with
Eq.

4.20
. When
\displaystyle p\geq 2
, estimate
Eq.

6.16
follows immediately with
\displaystyle\bar{\sigma}=\sigma_{0}
. On the other hand, when
\displaystyle p\in(1,2)
we estimate using
Eqs.

6.15
and
6.7
, obtaining

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\nabla\tilde{u}-\nabla\tilde{h}|^{p}dx&\leq&C\left(\fint_{B^{+}_{\varrho/4}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|V_{\mu}(\nabla\tilde{u})-V_{\mu}(\nabla\tilde{h})|^{2}dx\right)^{p/2}\left(\fint_{B_{\varrho/4}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}(|\nabla\tilde{u}|^{2}+|\nabla\tilde{h}|^{2}+\mu^{2})^{p/2}\right)^{\frac{2-p}{2}}\\
&\leq&C_{\lambda}\varrho^{\frac{\sigma_{0}p-(2-p)\lambda}{2}p},\end{array}

for every
\displaystyle\lambda>0
. Therefore, by choosing
\displaystyle\lambda:=\frac{\sigma_{0}p}{2(2-p)}
, we obtain the desired estimate
Eq.

6.16
with
\displaystyle\bar{\sigma}=\frac{\sigma_{0}p}{4}
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

2.21

Once
Eq.

6.16
is established, one can follow the same calculations of the proof of
Theorem

2.20
using
Eqs.

4.3
,
6.7
,
6.10
and
6.15
. Let
\displaystyle t\in\left(0,\frac{\varrho}{8}\right]
, with
\displaystyle\varrho\in(0,\frac{r_{0}}{4}]
. For every
\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{0}\in\Gamma_{r_{0}/2}
, we have

\fint_{B_{t}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\nabla\tilde{u}-(\nabla\tilde{u})_{B_{t}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{p}dx\leq C_{\sigma,\lambda}\left\{\left(\frac{\varrho}{t}\right)^{n}\varrho^{\bar{\sigma}p}+\left(\frac{t}{\varrho}\right)^{\bar{\alpha}_{0}p}\varrho^{-\lambda p}\right\},

for every
\displaystyle\lambda>0
. By choosing
\displaystyle t:=\frac{\varrho^{1+\frac{\bar{\sigma}p}{2n}}}{8}
and
\displaystyle\lambda:=\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\sigma}p}{4n}
, we then obtain

\fint_{B_{t}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\nabla\tilde{u}-(\nabla\tilde{u})_{B_{t}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{p}dx\leq Ct^{\gamma p}\quad\text{ for every }t\in\left(0,\varrho_{0}\right),

with
\displaystyle\varrho_{0}:=\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{4}\right)^{1+\frac{\bar{\sigma}p}{2n}}
and
\displaystyle\gamma:=\min\left\{\frac{n\bar{\sigma}}{2n+\bar{\sigma}p},\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{0}\bar{\sigma}p}{2(2n+\bar{\sigma}p)}\right\}
. This concludes up to relabeling
\displaystyle t
as
\displaystyle\varrho

{}\sup_{\tilde{x}_{0}\in\Gamma_{r_{0}/2}}\fint_{B_{\varrho}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|\nabla\tilde{u}-(\nabla\tilde{u})_{B_{\varrho}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{0})}|^{p}dx\leq C\varrho^{\gamma p},\quad\forall\varrho\in(0,\varrho_{0}),\text{ for some }\varrho_{0}\in(0,1).

(6.25)

By combining the interior Campanato estimate of [
24
, Theorem 3, Theorem 5] and the boundary estimate
Eq.

6.25
, the result follows via a standard covering argument and Campanato’s characterization of Hölder continuity.

Proof of
Theorem

2.22

As for
\displaystyle p\geq n
, embedding results imply
\displaystyle u\in L^{t}(\Omega)
for all
\displaystyle t\in[1,\infty)
and hence
\displaystyle f\in L^{d}(\Omega)
for some
\displaystyle d>n
, then the gradient regularity follows from [
1
, Theorem 1.1]. Therefore, without loss of generality, we assume
\displaystyle p<n
. The idea is to show
\displaystyle u\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
. By [
24
, Proposition 2.1], it is enough to show that
\displaystyle f\in L^{t}(\Omega)
for some
\displaystyle t>n/p
. Our method is by ideas found in
[
38
]
.

Step 1:
For
\displaystyle k>0
and
\displaystyle t\geq p
we define two
\displaystyle C^{1}
functions
\displaystyle h
and
\displaystyle\phi
on
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}
by

{}h(r)=\begin{cases}\operatorname{sign}(r)|r|^{t/p}&\text{ for }|r|\leq k,\\
\operatorname{sign}(r)\{(t/p)k^{t/p-1}|r|+(1-t/p)k^{t/p}\}&\text{ for }|r|>k,\end{cases}

(6.26)

and

{}\phi(r)=\int_{0}^{r}\left(h^{\prime}(\nu)\right)^{p}d\nu.

(6.27)

Clearly
\displaystyle\phi
is non-decreasing as
\displaystyle(h^{\prime})^{p}
is non-negative. As
\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}
is bounded,
\displaystyle\phi(u)\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
whenever
\displaystyle u\in W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)
. Let
\displaystyle v\in W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)
be any weak solution to

-\Delta_{p}v+(-\Delta)^{s}_{q}v+K(x)|v|^{p-2}v=F(x)\text{ in }\Omega;\quad v=0\text{ in }\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\Omega,

where
\displaystyle K,F\in L^{n/p}(\Omega)
. We show
\displaystyle v\in L^{t}(\Omega)
for every
\displaystyle t\in[1,\infty)
. Taking
\displaystyle\phi(v)
as a test function in the weak formulation, we get

{}\begin{array}[]{l}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla v|^{p}(h^{\prime}(v))^{p}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|v(x)-v(y)|^{q-2}(v(x)-v(y))(\phi(v(x))-\phi(v(y)))}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\\
\qquad+\int_{\Omega}K\phi(v)v|v|^{p-2}dx=\int_{\Omega}F\phi(v)dx.\end{array}

(6.28)

By [
16
, Lemma A.2]

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|v(x)-v(y)|^{q-2}(v(x)-v(y))(\phi(v(x))-\phi(v(y)))}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|G(u(x))-G(v(y))|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\geq 0,

where
\displaystyle G(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\phi^{\prime}(\tau)^{\frac{1}{q}}d\tau
. Therefore,
Eq.

6.28
implies

{}\begin{array}[]{l}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla v|^{p}(h^{\prime}(v))^{p}dx+\int_{\Omega}K\phi(v)v|v|^{p-2}dx\leq\int_{\Omega}F\phi(v)dx.\end{array}

(6.29)

For
\displaystyle m>0
set
\displaystyle\Omega_{m}=\{x\in\Omega:|K(x)|>m\}
. We then have

{}\left|\int_{\Omega}K\phi(v)v|v|^{p-2}dx\right|\leq m\int_{\Omega\backslash\Omega_{m}}|v|^{p-1}|\phi(v)|dx+\|K\|_{L^{n/p}(\Omega_{m})}\left(\int_{\Omega}||v|^{p-1}\phi(v)|^{n/(n-p)}dx\right)^{\frac{n-p}{n}}.

(6.30)

Moreover
\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla v|^{p}(h^{\prime}(v))^{p}dx=\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(h(v))|^{p}dx\geqq S\left(\int_{\Omega}|h(v)|^{p^{*}}dx\right)^{p/{p*}}
.
From the definition of
\displaystyle h
and
\displaystyle\phi
there exists
\displaystyle C
independent of
\displaystyle k
such that

{}||r|^{p-1}\phi(r)|\leq C|h(r)|^{p},\quad|\phi(r)|\leq C|h(r)|^{p(t+1-p)/t},

(6.31)

for any
\displaystyle r
. If we choose
\displaystyle m
such that
\displaystyle\|K\|_{L^{n/p}(\Omega_{m})}\leq\frac{S}{2C}
, we obtain from
Eq.

6.29
using
Eqs.

6.30
and
6.31

{}\frac{S}{2}\left(\int_{\Omega}|h(v)|^{p^{*}}dx\right)^{p/{p*}}\leq Cm\int_{\Omega}|h(v)|^{p}\mathrm{~d}x+C\|F\|_{L^{n/p}(\Omega)}\left(\int_{\Omega}|h(v)|^{p^{*}(t+1-p)/t}dx\right)^{p/p^{*}}

(6.32)

and

{}\left(\int_{\Omega}|h(v)|^{p^{*}(t+1-p)/t}dx\right)^{p/p^{*}}\leq|\Omega|^{p(p-1)/p^{*}t}\left(\int_{\Omega}|h(v)|^{p^{*}}dx\right)^{(t+1-p)p/tp^{*}}.

(6.33)

If we assume now that
\displaystyle v\in L^{t}(\Omega)
then
\displaystyle v\in L^{tn/(n-p)}(\Omega)
(by
Eqs.

6.32
and
6.33
) and

\frac{S}{2}\|v\|_{L^{tn/(n-p)}(\Omega)}^{t}\leq Cm\|v\|_{L^{t}(\Omega)}^{t}+C|\Omega|^{p(p-1)/p^{*}t}\|F\|_{L^{n/p}(\Omega)}\|v\|_{L^{tn/n-p}(\Omega)}^{t+1-p}.

With Young’s inequality, we obtain

\|v\|_{L^{tn/(n-p)}(\Omega)}\leq C_{1}\|v\|_{L^{t}(\Omega)}+C_{2}\|F\|_{L^{n/p}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{p-1}},

where
\displaystyle C_{1}
and
\displaystyle C_{2}
depend on
\displaystyle m,t,n,p
and
\displaystyle|\Omega|
. Define now

t_{0}=p,\quad t_{l}=\left(\frac{n}{n-p}\right)^{l}p,\quad l\in\mathbb{N}^{*}

and get
\displaystyle v\in L^{t_{l}}(\Omega)
for any
\displaystyle l\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Therefore
\displaystyle v\in L^{t}(\Omega)
for all
\displaystyle t\in[1,\infty)
.

Step 2:
Set
\displaystyle K(x)=\operatorname{sign}(u)f(x,u)/(1+|u|^{p-1}).
Then
\displaystyle K
satisfies

|K(x)|\leq C_{0}(1+|u|^{p^{*}-1})/(1+|u|^{p-1})\leq M_{1}|u|^{p^{*}-p}+M_{2},

\displaystyle M_{1},M_{2}
being constants. As
\displaystyle p^{*}-p=p^{2}/(n-p)
and
\displaystyle u\in L^{p^{*}}(\Omega)
we deduce that
\displaystyle K\in L^{n/p}(\Omega)
. Then
Eq.

2.12
can be written in the form

-\Delta_{p}u+(-\Delta)^{s}_{q}u=K(x)|u|^{p-2}u+\operatorname{sign}(u)K(x).

By step 1, we deduce that
\displaystyle u\in\bigcap_{1\leq t<+\infty}L^{t}(\Omega)
. Hence
\displaystyle K|u|^{p-2}u+\operatorname{sign}(u)K\in\bigcap_{1\leq t<+\infty}L^{t}(\Omega)
, and from [
24
, proposition 2.1] we deduce that
\displaystyle u\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
. Finally by [
1
, Theorem 1.1] we get the
\displaystyle C^{1,\gamma}
regularity of
\displaystyle u
in
\displaystyle\overline{\Omega}
.

7
Singular problems

We now turn to singular problems. For this, first we consider the general problem for
\displaystyle 1<q\leq p<\infty

{}\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{L}u=f(x)u^{-\delta}\text{ in }\Omega,u>0\text{ in }\Omega,u=0\text{ in }\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\Omega;\end{array}

(7.1)

where
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}
is given by
Eq.

2.5
and satisfies the growth conditions
Eqs.

2.6
,
2.7
,
2.8
and
2.9
with
\displaystyle\bar{\alpha}=1,\mu=0
in
Eq.

2.6
,
\displaystyle\delta>0
and
\displaystyle f
satisfies the condition
Eq.

2.14
for some
\displaystyle\beta\geq 0
. We show the existence of weak solutions and then take the particular operator
\displaystyle\mathcal{L}u=-\Delta_{p}u+(-\Delta)_{q}^{s}u
to prove
Theorem

2.27
to
Theorem

2.31
. As a result of
Eq.

2.6
it holds (see [
24
, section 2.3]) for some
\displaystyle c>0

{}|z|^{p}\leq cA(x,z)\cdot z

(7.2)

for all
\displaystyle x\in\Omega
and
\displaystyle z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. We follow the approximation method in
[
14
]
. To this end, we investigate the approximating problem

{}-\operatorname{div}A(x,\nabla u(x))+\text{ P.V. }\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Phi(u(x)-u(y))\frac{B(x,y)}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dy=f_{\epsilon}(x)(u^{+}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}\text{ in }\Omega,\quad u=0\text{ on }\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash\Omega,

(7.3)

where

f_{\epsilon}(x):=\begin{cases}\left(f(x)^{\frac{-1}{\beta}}+\epsilon^{\frac{p-1+\delta}{p-\beta}}\right)^{-\beta}&\text{ if }f(x)>0\\
0&\text{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

It is easy to observe that, for
\displaystyle\beta<p
, the function
\displaystyle f_{\epsilon}
increases as
\displaystyle\epsilon\downarrow 0
and
\displaystyle f_{\epsilon}\leq f
for all
\displaystyle\epsilon>0
.

Lemma 7.1
.

(a) For every
\displaystyle\epsilon>0
, problem
Eq.

7.3
admits a positive weak solution
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)\cap L^{\infty}(\Omega)
.
(b) For
\displaystyle\beta<p
, the
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}
increases as
\displaystyle\epsilon\downarrow 0
and
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}
is unique for every
\displaystyle\epsilon>0
.
(c) For every
\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}\subset\subset\Omega
, there exists a positive constant
\displaystyle C_{\Omega^{\prime}}
such that
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\geq C_{\Omega^{\prime}}>0
in
\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime},\,\forall\epsilon\in(0,1]
. More specifically,
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\geq Cd(x,\partial\Omega)>0
in
\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime},\,\forall\epsilon\in(0,1]
.

Proof.

Let
\displaystyle V=W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
with the norm
\displaystyle\|u\|=\|\nabla u\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}
and let
\displaystyle V^{\prime}
be the dual of
\displaystyle V
. Note [
24
, Section 2.5] and for every
\displaystyle\epsilon>0
, define
\displaystyle T:V\rightarrow V^{\prime}
by

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\langle T(v),\phi\rangle&=&\int_{\Omega}A(x,\nabla v(x))\cdot\nabla\phi(x)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|v(x)-v(y)|^{q-2}(v(x)-v(y))(\phi(x)-\phi(y))}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\\
&&-\int_{\Omega}f_{\epsilon}(x)(v^{+}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}\phi(x)dx,\end{array}

for every
\displaystyle v,\phi\in V
. By
Eq.

2.6
,
\displaystyle T
is
well defined
. Using
Eq.

2.6
, Hölder’s inequality,
Lemma

2.4
and
Theorem

2.9
,

|\langle T(v),\phi\rangle|\leq C\int_{\Omega}|\nabla v|^{p-1}|\nabla\phi|dx+C[v]_{s,q}^{q-1}[\phi]_{s,q}+C(\epsilon)\int_{\Omega}|\phi|dx\leq C(\|v\|^{p-1}\|\phi\|+[v]_{s,q}^{q-1}\|\phi\|+\|\phi\|)\leq C|\phi|,

for some constant
\displaystyle C
depending on
\displaystyle\epsilon
. Now by Hölder’s inequality,
Eq.

7.2
and
Theorem

2.9
, we obtain

\langle T(v),v\rangle=\int_{\Omega}A(x,\nabla v)\cdot\nabla v\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|v(x)-v(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy-\int_{\Omega}f_{\epsilon}(v^{+}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}v\,dx\geq C_{1}\|v\|^{p}-C\|v\|.

Since
\displaystyle 1<p<\infty
, we may conclude that
\displaystyle T
is
coercive
. We now show
\displaystyle T
is demicontinuous. Let
\displaystyle v_{k}\rightarrow v
in the norm of
\displaystyle V
as
\displaystyle k\rightarrow\infty
. Then
\displaystyle\nabla v_{k}\rightarrow\nabla v
in
\displaystyle L^{p}(\Omega)
as
\displaystyle k\rightarrow\infty
. Therefore, up to a subsequence, still denoted by
\displaystyle v_{k}
, we have
\displaystyle v_{k}(x)\rightarrow v(x)
and
\displaystyle\nabla v_{k}(x)\rightarrow\nabla v(x)
pointwise for almost every
\displaystyle x\in\Omega
. Since the function
\displaystyle A(x,\cdot)
is continuous in the second variable, we have

\displaystyle A(x,\nabla v_{k})\rightarrow A(x,\nabla v)

pointwise for almost every
\displaystyle x\in\Omega
as
\displaystyle k\rightarrow\infty
. By
Eq.

2.6
and the norm boundedness of the sequence
\displaystyle\{v_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, we obtain

\|A(x,\nabla v_{k})\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{p^{\prime}}=\int_{\Omega}|A(x,\nabla v_{k})|^{p^{\prime}}dx\leq C\int_{\Omega}|\nabla v_{k}(x)|^{p}dx\leq C,

for some positive constant
\displaystyle C
independent of
\displaystyle k
. Therefore, up to a subsequence

\displaystyle A(x,\nabla v_{k}(x))\rightharpoonup A(x,\nabla v(x))

weakly in
\displaystyle L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
as
\displaystyle k\rightarrow\infty
, and since the weak limit is independent of the choice of the subsequence, the above weak convergence actually holds for the original sequence. As
\displaystyle\phi\in V
implies that
\displaystyle\nabla\phi\in L^{p}(\Omega)
, by the weak convergence, we obtain

{}\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\Omega}A(x,\nabla v_{k}(x))\cdot\nabla\phi(x)dx=\int_{\Omega}A(x,\nabla v(x))\cdot\nabla\phi(x)dx.

(7.4)

Since
\displaystyle\{v_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a bounded sequence in
\displaystyle V
and
\displaystyle q\leq p
,
Lemma

2.4
implies that
\displaystyle\left\{\frac{|v_{k}(x)-v_{k}(y)|^{q-2}(v_{k}(x)-v_{k}(y))}{|x-y|^{\frac{(n+sq)(q-1)}{q}}}\right\}
is a bounded sequence in
\displaystyle L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Since
\displaystyle\phi\in V
, we have

\displaystyle(\phi(x)-\phi(y))|x-y|^{-\frac{n+sq}{q}}\in L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}).

Therefore, by the weak convergence, we have

{}\begin{array}[]{c}\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|v_{k}(x)-v_{k}(y)|^{q-2}(v_{k}(x)-v_{k}(y))(\phi(x)-\phi(y))}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\\
=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|v(x)-v(y)|^{q-2}(v(x)-v(y))(\phi(x)-\phi(y))}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy.\end{array}

(7.5)

Finally, as
\displaystyle v_{k}(x)\rightarrow v(x)
pointwise almost everywhere in
\displaystyle\Omega
and

\left|f_{\epsilon}(x)(v_{k}^{+}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}\phi(x)-f_{\epsilon}(x)(v^{+}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}\phi(x)\right|\leq C(\epsilon)|\phi(x)|,

for every
\displaystyle\phi\in V
. By the dominated convergence theorem, we have

{}\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\Omega}f_{\epsilon}(x)(v_{k}^{+}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}\phi(x)=\int_{\Omega}f_{\epsilon}(x)(v^{+}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}\phi(x),

(7.6)

for every
\displaystyle\phi\in V
and
\displaystyle\epsilon>0
. Merging
Eqs.

7.4
,
7.5
and
7.6
it follows that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left(T\left(v_{k}\right),\phi\right\rangle=\langle T(v),\phi\rangle,

for every
\displaystyle\phi\in V
, and hence
\displaystyle T
is
demicontinuous
. Finally, we show the monotonicity of
\displaystyle T
. Let
\displaystyle v_{1},v_{2}\in V
. Using
Lemma

2.14
and
Eq.

4.21
it is easy to observe that

\left\langle T(v_{1})-T(v_{2}),v_{1}-v_{2}\right\rangle\geq-\int_{{\Omega}}f_{\epsilon}(x)\left((v_{1}^{+}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}-(v_{2}^{+}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}\right)(v_{1}(x)-v_{2}(x))dx.

One can easily check that the integral present on the right is nonpositive. This shows that
\displaystyle T
is
monotone
. As a result, by [
21
, Theorem 9.14], we conclude
\displaystyle T
is surjective. Hence for every
\displaystyle\epsilon>0
, there exists
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\in V
such that

\int_{\Omega}A(x,\nabla u_{\epsilon})\cdot\nabla\phi\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon}(y)|^{q-2}(u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon}(y))(\phi(x)-\phi(y))}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy=\int_{\Omega}f_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon}^{+}+\epsilon)^{-\delta}\phi\,dx,

for every
\displaystyle\phi\in V
. This shows that
Eq.

7.3
has a weak solution
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
for every
\displaystyle\epsilon>0
. Using
Eq.

7.2
and proceeding along the lines of the proof of [
34
, Lemma 3.1], it follows that
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
for every
\displaystyle\epsilon>0
. Now let

\displaystyle g_{\epsilon}(x)=f_{\epsilon}(x)(u_{\epsilon}^{+}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}.

Choosing
\displaystyle\phi=\min\{u_{\epsilon},0\}=(u_{\epsilon})_{-}
as a test function and using the non-negativity of
\displaystyle g_{\epsilon}
, we have by
Eq.

7.2

C\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(u_{\epsilon})_{-})|^{p}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon}(y)|^{q-2}(u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon}(y))((u_{\epsilon}(x))_{-}-(u_{\epsilon}(y))_{-})}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy=\int_{\Omega}g_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon})_{-}dx\leq 0.

Proceeding similarly to the proof of the estimate [
34
, (3.13)], the second integral above is nonnegative. Hence

\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(u_{\epsilon})_{-})|^{p}dx=0,

which gives
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\geq 0
in
\displaystyle\Omega
.

To show the monotonicity, let
\displaystyle 0<\epsilon^{\prime}\leq\epsilon
, we choose
\displaystyle\phi=(u_{\epsilon}-u_{\epsilon^{\prime}})^{+}
as a test function in
Eq.

7.3
to have

{}\begin{array}[]{l}\int_{\Omega}(A(x,\nabla u_{\epsilon}(x))-A(x,\nabla u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x)))\cdot\nabla(u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x))^{+}dx\\
+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\mathcal{A}(u_{\epsilon}(x,y))-\mathcal{A}(u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x,y)))((u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x))^{+}-(u_{\epsilon}(y)-u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(y))^{+})d\mu\\
=\int_{\Omega}(f_{\epsilon}(x)(u_{\epsilon}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}-f_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x)(u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x)+\epsilon^{\prime})^{-\delta})(u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x))^{+}dx\\
\leq\int_{\Omega}f_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x)((u_{\epsilon}(x)+\epsilon)^{-\delta}-(u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x)+\epsilon^{\prime})^{-\delta})(u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x))^{+}dx\leq 0,\end{array}

(7.7)

where
\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(g(x,y))=|g(x)-g(y)|^{q-2}(g(x)-g(y))
,
\displaystyle d\mu=\frac{dxdy}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}
and the last inequality above follows using
\displaystyle f_{\epsilon}\leq f_{\epsilon^{\prime}}
for
\displaystyle\beta<p
. Following the same arguments from the proof of [
42
, Lemma 9], we obtain

{}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\mathcal{A}(u_{\epsilon}(x,y))-\mathcal{A}(u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x,y)))((u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x))^{+}-(u_{\epsilon}(y)-u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(y))^{+})d\mu\geq 0.

(7.8)

Merging
Eqs.

7.7
and
7.8
, one gets

\int_{\Omega}(A(x,\nabla u_{\epsilon}(x))-A(x,\nabla u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x)))\cdot\nabla(u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x))^{+}dx\leq 0.

Thus, by
Eqs.

4.20
and
4.21
, we may conclude that

\displaystyle\nabla u_{\epsilon}=\nabla u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\text{ in }\{x\in\Omega:u_{\epsilon}(x)>u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(x)\}.

Hence, we have
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\geq u_{\epsilon}
in
\displaystyle\Omega
. The uniqueness of
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}
follows similarly. Finally, by
Theorem

2.21
(note that the condition on
\displaystyle u
to be less than distance function is not needed if
\displaystyle f
is bounded in
Theorem

2.21
), it follows,
\displaystyle u_{1}\in C^{1,\gamma}(\overline{\Omega})
for some
\displaystyle\gamma\in(0,1)
. Since
\displaystyle g_{1}\neq 0
in
\displaystyle\Omega
, by the strong maximum principle [
1
, Proposition 6.1], it holds
\displaystyle u_{1}>0
. Therefore, for every
\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}\subset\subset\Omega
, there exists a positive constant
\displaystyle C=C(\Omega^{\prime})
such that
\displaystyle u_{1}\geq C(\Omega^{\prime})>0
in
\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}
. Using the monotonicity property, we get
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\geq u_{1}
in
\displaystyle\Omega
, and thus, for every
\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}\subset\subset\Omega
, there exists a positive constant
\displaystyle C(\Omega^{\prime})
(independent of
\displaystyle\epsilon
) such that
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\geq C(\Omega^{\prime})>0
in
\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}
. In fact, by [
1
, Corollary 1.3], it holds
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\geq u_{1}\geq Cd(x,\partial\Omega)>0
in
\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime},\,\forall\epsilon\in(0,1]
, where
\displaystyle C>0
is a constant independent of
\displaystyle\epsilon
.
∎

From now on, we will denote the distance of
\displaystyle x\in\Omega
from the boundary
\displaystyle\partial\Omega
by
\displaystyle d(x)
and in short, we write
\displaystyle d
.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\displaystyle\beta\in[0,p)
. The sequence
\displaystyle\{u_{\epsilon}\}
is bounded in
\displaystyle W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
if
\displaystyle\beta+\delta\leq 1
, and the sequence
\displaystyle\{u_{\epsilon}^{\theta}\}
is bounded in
\displaystyle W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
if
\displaystyle\beta+\delta>1
, for some
\displaystyle\theta>1
. Also in the later case
\displaystyle\{u_{\epsilon}\}
is uniformly bounded in
\displaystyle W^{1.p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
.

Proof.

For the case
\displaystyle\beta+\delta\leq 1
, testing the weak formulation of the
Eq.

7.3
by
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}
, and using
Eqs.

2.7
,
2.8
,
7.2
and
2.14
together with the Hardy’s inequality, we get

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\|u_{\epsilon}\|^{p}_{W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)}&\leq&c\int_{\Omega}A(x,\nabla u_{\epsilon})\cdot\nabla u_{\epsilon}\,dx+c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon}(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\\
&=&c\int_{\Omega}f_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon}+\epsilon)^{-\delta}u_{\epsilon}\,dx\leq C\int_{\Omega}d^{1-\beta-\delta}\left(\frac{u_{\epsilon}}{d}\right)^{1-\delta}\leq C\|u_{\epsilon}\|_{W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{1-\delta}\text{, }\end{array}

where
\displaystyle C
is independent of
\displaystyle\epsilon
. We have also used the fact that
\displaystyle 0\leq f_{\epsilon}\leq f
for every
\displaystyle\epsilon>0
. For the remaining case, we note [
24
, Section 2.5] and test the weak formulation of the problem by
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}^{m}
, where
\displaystyle m=p(\theta-1)+1
for some
\displaystyle\theta\geq 1
(to be specified later) to get

\begin{array}[]{l}\|u_{\epsilon}^{\theta}\|^{p}_{W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)}\leq c\int_{\Omega}A(x,\nabla u_{\epsilon})\cdot\nabla u_{\epsilon}^{m}\,dx+c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}{\mathcal{A}u_{\epsilon}(x,y)(u_{\epsilon}^{m}(x)-u^{m}_{\epsilon}(y))}d\mu=c\int_{\Omega}f_{\epsilon}(u_{\epsilon}+\epsilon)^{-\delta}u_{\epsilon}^{m}dx,\end{array}

where
\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(v(x,y))=|v(x)-v(y)|^{q-2}(v(x)-v(y))
,
\displaystyle d\mu=\frac{dxdy}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}
. We now use [
16
, Lemma A.2] for

\displaystyle g(t):=t^{p(\theta-1)+1},

\displaystyle G(t):=\int_{0}^{t}g^{\prime}(\tau)^{1/q}d\tau,
to get that the nonlocal term

\begin{array}[]{l}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon}(y)|^{q-2}(u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon}(y))(u_{\epsilon}^{p(\theta-1)+1}(x)-u^{p(\theta-1)+1}_{\epsilon}(y))}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\\
\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|G(u_{\epsilon}(x))-G(u_{\epsilon}(y))|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy\geq 0,\end{array}

which implies, on account of Hölder’s and Hardy’s inequalities,

\|u_{\epsilon}^{\theta}\|^{p}_{W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)}\leq C\int_{\Omega}d^{-\beta+\frac{p(\theta-1)+1-\delta}{\theta}}\left(\frac{u_{\epsilon}^{\theta}}{d}\right)^{\frac{p(\theta-1)+1-\delta}{\theta}}\leq C\|u_{\epsilon}^{\theta}\|_{W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{\frac{p(\theta-1)+1-\delta}{\theta}}\text{, }

where

{}\theta>\max\left\{1,\frac{(p+\delta-1)(p-1)}{p(p-\beta)},\frac{p+\delta-1}{p}\right\}

(7.9)

and
\displaystyle C>0
is independent of
\displaystyle\epsilon
. This concludes that the sequence
\displaystyle\{u_{\epsilon}^{\theta}\}
is bounded in
\displaystyle W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
. Follow now the arguments of [
34
, Lemma 3.7] (taking
\displaystyle\delta+p-1/p=\theta
there) to get
\displaystyle\{u_{\epsilon}\}
is uniformly bounded in
\displaystyle W^{1.p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
.
∎

Remark 7.3
.

Let
\displaystyle\beta\in[0,p)
. In view of
Lemma

7.2
, we can say that up to a subsequence
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\rightharpoonup u
(or
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}^{\theta}\rightharpoonup u^{\theta}
) weakly in
\displaystyle W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)
and strongly in
\displaystyle L^{p}(\Omega)
if
\displaystyle\beta+\delta\leq 1
(or
\displaystyle\beta+\delta>1
), as
\displaystyle\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, for some
\displaystyle u\in W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)
(or
\displaystyle u^{\theta}\in W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)
). Also in each case
\displaystyle u_{\epsilon}\rightharpoonup u
weakly in
\displaystyle W^{1,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
. As
\displaystyle\{u_{\epsilon}\}
is monotone, we can treat
\displaystyle u
as the pointwise supremum of
\displaystyle\{u_{\epsilon}\}
and hence
\displaystyle u\geq u_{\epsilon}\geq 0
, for every
\displaystyle\epsilon>0
. Further for any
\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}\subset\subset\Omega
, there exists
\displaystyle C_{\Omega^{\prime}}>0
such that
\displaystyle u(x)\geq u_{\epsilon}(x)\geq C_{\Omega^{\prime}}d(x)>0
in
\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}
for all
\displaystyle\epsilon\in(0,1]
.

We now give the result regarding the convergence of gradients of
\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon}
to the gradient of
\displaystyle u
a.e. in
\displaystyle\Omega
.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
\displaystyle\beta\in[0,p)
. Take
\displaystyle u
of
Remark

7.3
. Then up to a subsequence,
\displaystyle\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\nabla u
pointwise a.e. in
\displaystyle\Omega
.

Proof.

Let us take a compact
\displaystyle K\subset\Omega
and consider a function
\displaystyle\phi_{K}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
such that
\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}\phi_{K}=\omega
,
\displaystyle 0\leq\phi_{K}\leq 1
in
\displaystyle\Omega
and
\displaystyle\phi_{K}\equiv 1
in
\displaystyle K
. Now for
\displaystyle\mu>0
, we define the truncated function
\displaystyle T_{\mu}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

T_{\mu}(t)=\begin{cases}t,&\text{ if }|t|\leq\mu,\\
\mu\frac{t}{|t|},&\text{ if }|t|>\mu.\end{cases}

Now choose
\displaystyle\psi_{\epsilon}=\phi_{K}T_{\mu}((u_{\epsilon}-u))\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
as a test function in
Eq.

7.3
and note [
24
, Section 2.5], to get

{}I+J=R,

(7.10)

where

I=\int_{\Omega}A(x,\nabla u_{\epsilon})\cdot\nabla\psi_{\epsilon}\,dx,J=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}\frac{|u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon}(y)|^{q-2}(u_{\epsilon}(x)-u_{\epsilon}(y))(\psi_{\epsilon}(x)-\psi_{\epsilon}(y))}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dxdy,R=\int_{\Omega}\frac{f_{\epsilon}\psi_{\epsilon}}{(u_{\epsilon}+\epsilon)^{\delta}}dx.

Estimate of
\displaystyle I
:
We have
